Mortgage lock in decay is measurable, the annual unlock rate is 5.8%

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Mortgage lock in is suppressing turnover, with existing sales near 4.2 million versus a 5 million norm. The article lays out 4 life event driven channels that can unlock listings, with a cited 5.8% annual decay rate.  AI Summary

Mortgage lock-in is real and measurable. It is also decaying on its own, because death, divorce, diapers, and diplomas keep arriving on schedule. Here is how to work that schedule.

There is a number floating around this fall that explains the mood in most sales meetings. Research by Compass economist Jonah Coste calculates that mortgage rate lock-in is preventing about 870,000 home sales in 2026 that would otherwise take place, with existing home sales running near 4.2 million against a long-run norm of 5 million. With the 30-year rate at 7.35% as of the November 12, nobody in the room expects relief soon.

Read that same research one paragraph further though, and you find the sentence a real estate professional should tape to the dashboard. About 5.8% of locked-in homes get unlocked every year through what the economists call normal demographic and economic activity. That is their phrase for it. This business has always had a shorter one. Death, divorce, diapers and diplomas. The four D’s.

Those four events are the reason lock-in decays whether rates fall or not. They are not a forecast. They are a tide chart.

A charter captain does not cancel the season because the weather turned. He fishes the tide, because the tide table was printed a year ago, and the tide shows up on time no matter what the sky is doing. The rate is the weather. The four D’s are the tide. An agent who only works when the weather is pleasant will spend most of this cycle tied to the dock. The one who learns to read the table will come back with a full boat while everyone else is refreshing the forecast.

Here is the table, one ‘D’ at a time: where the list lives, and what to bring when you show up.

Every estate holding real property ends in a sale or a transfer, and the people managing it are almost always doing it for the first time.

The List: Probate court filings, which are public, county-level and frequently online.

The Better List: The attorneys who file them. Estate attorneys, elder law attorneys, trust administrators. Add the liquidation companies and the move managers who empty the house before anyone thinks to call an agent.

What to bring: A valuation as of the date of death, at no charge, on any file where the attorney needs one. Every estate requires that figure for tax basis and most agents have never thought to offer it. Be the one who does. Do not contact a family off an obituary, ever. The professionals around the family are the door. Walk through it with service in your hands rather than a business card.

A divorce splits a household in half, and the house seldom fits either half. What makes these listings difficult is not pricing or marketing. It is that both spouses must agree on you.

The List: Family law attorneys, mediators, and the planners who specialize in divorce finances. They know about the house roughly six months before the MLS does.

What to bring. Neutrality in writing. A single page the attorney can give both parties explaining how the sale works, how proceeds are typically held, and what the timeline looks like. Offer to meet each spouse on their own. An attorney’s biggest worry is an agent who picks a side. Remove that worry and you become the standing referral for the whole practice.

No rate on earth holds a family in a two-bedroom once the second child arrives. The lock-in researchers can model payment shock with precision. They cannot model a crib in the dining room.

The List: Your own past clients first: every buyer you placed in a small home or condo three to five years back. Then the places young parents already spend their Saturdays, pediatric offices, daycare centers, parent groups, library story time.

What to bring. Your presence rather than your postcard. Sponsor the daycare fundraiser. Show up for the T-ball opener. One Fair Housing note that carries real consequences: advertise the house and the service, never who ought to live there, and have your broker review any audience targeting before it runs.

The August a family’s last child leaves is the August a four-bedroom colonial becomes three empty rooms and a tax bill. Parents feel it at once. They do something about it the next spring.

The List: Graduating senior names are still printed locally every June. Match those surnames to homes bought 18 to 25 years ago in the assessor’s records. What you are holding is a roster of parents about to stand in an empty bedroom wondering what comes next.

What to bring. An empty-nest conversation. Reserve the library meeting room, invite a financial planner, and talk about what happens to a house that got too big. No slides, no listing presentation, no pressure of any kind. Treat people like people in September and they call you in April.

“I’m not reaching out because you have to sell. I’m reaching out so that when life changes, and it changes for all of us, you already know somebody. No presentation and no ask. Only a name worth holding onto.”

Then stop talking. That is a conversation rather than a sales call, and it works across all four D’s because it is honest about who you are and what you want. Serve first, and the listing takes care of itself.

The agents waiting on a rate cut are watching the weather. The agents working the four D’s are fishing the tide, and the tide, unlike the weather, has never once missed an appointment.

He has trained hundreds of thousands of real estate professionals worldwide and leads the POWER AGENT® Coaching Program. Learn more at darrylspeaks.com.

Ethereum Price at $2,000: Scoop the Dip, Hold, or Dump?

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Ahmed Barakat is a journalist and copywriter based in Georgia with a growing focus on blockchain technology, DeFi, AI, privacy, digital assets, and fintech innovation.

The CryptoNews editorial team is composed of seasoned writers specializing in cryptocurrency and blockchain technology. Their expertise ensures comprehensive, accurate, and insightful content for...

Ethereum price is testing investors’ patience once again. Its latest recovery attempt falls apart. After giving back its August gains, ETH has returned to the $1,900–$2,050 zone, putting a critical breakout area at risk.

The $2,000 level is the market’s battleground. Buyers need to defend this area before another wave of selling pushes Ethereum lower. On the other hand, traders are increasingly looking toward $2,200 as the threshold for a meaningful recovery. Until ETH reclaims that level, any bounce risks becoming another temporary relief rally.So, buy, hold, or time to sell?

Ethereum’s latest decline has erased much of the optimism surrounding its August recovery. The second-largest crypto has slipped back toward the price region that previously supported its breakout.

That makes the current setup particularly important. The $1,900–$2,050 range previously acted as resistance before buyers pushed ETH higher. But can this resistance become support?

The technical picture remains uncomfortable for bulls. Ethereum has continued forming lower highs, and it can be interpreted that sellers are still controlling the short-term trend. A move below $2,000 would put the lower end of the previous breakout base under pressure. The $1,900 area would then become an important downside level to watch.

Discover: The Crypto Presales This Q4 2026

Ethereum investors now face three competing strategies as ETH revisits a previous breakout area. ETH is not trading at an unfamiliar support level. If buyers defend $2,000, bargain hunters could view the correction as an opportunity to accumulate.

Long-term holders may prefer to wait. Ethereum’s underlying ecosystem continues developing, with experiments involving zETH and Activity Markets bringing fresh attention to on-chain participation.

These projects explore ways to measure and reward Ethereum activity, including trading, lending, borrowing, staking, and restaking. However, their development has yet to translate into a clear reversal in ETH’s price momentum. The $2,200 threshold is arguably more important than the initial bounce from $2,000.

$ETH is currently in a multi-year accumulation phase.Sooner or later, this pattern will break out to the upside, and when that happens, I expect a massive price surge.It’s not a question of if, but when.Make a note of this tweet.

Reclaiming $2,200 would provide stronger evidence that buyers are regaining control. It would also help invalidate the immediate bearish structure created by successive lower highs. Until this happens, aggressive dip-buying carries the risk of catching a falling market.

The bearish scenario becomes more convincing if Ethereum loses the $2,000 price level decisively. A breakdown could expose $1,950 before bringing the $1,850 support in view.

Ethereum has two important levels to watch. $2,000 could determine whether buyers can prevent another breakdown, while $2,200 could confirm whether the recovery has enough strength to continue.

Ethereum isn’t the only crypto opportunity attracting investors. Bitcoin Hyper ($HYPER) is counting down to its November 26 token launch, with just 14 days remaining for early participants to secure tokens through its presale.

The project has raised more than $33.2 million, with its presale price listed at just $0.0136. Its staking program also offers rewards of up to 35% APY, giving early buyers another incentive to accumulate before launch.

Bitcoin Hyper aims to bring faster transactions and smart contract functionality to Bitcoin using the Solana Virtual Machine (SVM). Its architecture combines a Bitcoin bridge, Layer 2 execution, and zero-knowledge proofs to support decentralized applications.

Developers are already working on the project’s public testnet infrastructure, including an RPC endpoint, block explorer, wallet functionality, and bridge components. The goal is to create an ecosystem where developers can build applications while leveraging Bitcoin’s underlying network.

There is a parallel with Ethereum’s early days. ETH eventually grew beyond its original payment-token narrative by attracting developers, applications, and liquidity. Bitcoin Hyper is pursuing a similar ecosystem-building strategy around Bitcoin.

HYPER isn’t competing with Ethereum’s market size today. That’s precisely where its speculative appeal lies. A smaller starting valuation could offer greater percentage-return potential if adoption accelerates, while staking rewards provide an additional attraction for early participants.

With November 26 approaching, the presale countdown is adding urgency to a project betting on Bitcoin’s next evolution.

Rumble (RUM) Sells Two-Thirds of Its Bitcoin as AI Cloud Business Delivers First Profitable Quarter

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

RUM Group held 94.68 bitcoin worth $7.9 million on Sept. 30, down from 293.14 at the end of June, as its Quake AI unit drove record revenue of $96.6 million.

RUM Group, the parent of video platform Rumble, cut its bitcoin holdings by about two-thirds in the third quarter, the company disclosed Wednesday, as its newly acquired AI cloud business pushed it to its first profitable quarter on an adjusted basis.

The Nasdaq-listed company held 94.68 bitcoin, valued at $7.9 million, as of Sept. 30. That compares with 293.14 bitcoin worth about $17.2 million at the end of June, according to its second-quarter results.

That implies Rumble parted with roughly 198 bitcoin during the quarter, or about 68% of its stack.

The company’s cash-flow statement shows a net sale of digital assets of about $15.3 million for the first nine months of 2026. In the same period a year earlier, it spent $19.1 million buying digital assets.

Rumble did not explain the sales in its release, and it did not say what price it received. The balance sheet’s digital assets line, which the release does not break down, fell to $11.1 million from $18.5 million at the end of 2025.

The bitcoin drawdown came in a quarter dominated by Quake AI, the cloud and AI infrastructure unit Rumble built around Northern Data, the German GPU operator it acquired in June.

Revenue rose 290% from a year earlier to a record $96.6 million, beating the top of the company’s guidance range. Quake AI contributed $68.2 million of that, more than 70% of the total, in its first full quarter. The unit runs about 22,000 Nvidia GPUs, which the company said were about 85% utilized.

Adjusted EBITDA came in at $4.4 million, the first positive result in company history. On a GAAP basis, RUM Group still lost $89.9 million, weighed down by $85.2 million in amortization and depreciation. Executives said on the call that about $39 million of that came from writing up the value of Northern Data’s assets in purchase accounting.

“Quake AI is now the engine of our business,” CEO Chris Pavlovski said. “We have real assets running real AI workloads for real customers and generating real cash.”

The video business itself grew more slowly. Rumble’s video segment brought in $28.4 million, up 15% year over year, and posted a $12.1 million adjusted EBITDA loss, wider than a year earlier, executives said on the call.

Cash has been falling as well. RUM Group ended September with $134.4 million in cash and cash equivalents, down from $203.3 million at the end of June and $237.9 million at the end of 2025. The company raised its stake in Northern Data to about 98% during the quarter and expects to own all of it in early 2027.

It also warned that part of the roughly 250 megawatts of unmonetized capacity it had targeted for 2027 may slip beyond that year due to market conditions. RUM Group guided fourth-quarter revenue to $88 million to $94 million.

Rumble has been one of the more visible bitcoin-friendly platforms outside the crypto industry. In January, it launched a crypto wallet with Tether that lets creators receive payments in bitcoin.

Its sales add to a run of companies trimming or exiting bitcoin positions as prices fall and capital shifts toward AI. Hyperscale Data sold its last bitcoin to fund an AI data center, Keel sold its entire treasury for $192 million and MARA sold 13,597 bitcoin this month.

Bitcoin traded near $72,800 early Thursday, well below the roughly $83,400 per coin implied by Rumble’s Sept. 30 valuation. At that price, its remaining 94.68 bitcoin would be worth about $6.9 million.

Whether Rumble keeps a token stack or sells the rest is a question its next quarterly report should answer.

J.Crew Launches Claude-Powered Style Advisor for Outfit Shopping

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

J.Crew launched Style Advisor on November 12, 2026, an AI stylist on JCrew.com that recommends complete outfits instead of requiring separate searches for individual items. Anthropic said the U.S. launch covers women’s and men’s styles and uses multiple Claude models, with each assigned a different role.

A shopper can describe what is needed for a beach wedding or a first job interview, receive complete looks, and keep refining the suggestions from there. The interaction is designed around assembling an outfit, giving J.Crew a conversational route into shopping trips that might otherwise involve separate searches for a sweater, pants, and shoes.

Every recommendation begins with an outfit J.Crew stylists created for the brand’s catalog shoots. Style Advisor displays the photographed looks so customers can see the full combination. Customers can start with one piece and request the rest of its look, or ask for recommendations tied to an event.

That foundation shapes how the system represents the brand. J.Crew says its stylists have spent more than 40 years balancing proportion, color, and layering and making unexpected pairings. Style Advisor applies the principles a J.Crew stylist would use in a store, while the looks themselves originate with the company’s team. The AI therefore guides customers through brand-created outfits rather than serving as the source of those outfits.

J.Crew Group CEO Libby Wadle said the collaboration brings together the retailer’s styling, merchandising, and creative expertise to move “beyond individual product searches toward a more connected and inspiration-led experience.”

J.Crew’s product team used Claude Fable 5.1, which Anthropic describes as its model for complex, open-ended work, to plan how Style Advisor should reason like a stylist. The team then built a working prototype with Claude Opus 5.5 in Claude Code, and J.Crew’s engineers took that prototype to production.

The live service divides the work differently. Claude Sonnet 5.5 acts as the stylist and decides each step of a response, while Claude Haiku 5.5 handles quick, focused tasks, a split Anthropic says helps Style Advisor respond quickly. The setup assigns models to distinct jobs across planning, prototyping, conversation, and task execution rather than relying on one model throughout.

At launch, Style Advisor is focused on outfit ideas and shopping confidence, not recommendations individualized through a customer’s preferences and purchase history. J.Crew plans to add that personalization over time and expand the tool to additional markets and product lines.

Those plans outline a broader role for the system, but the current release establishes a narrower enterprise use case: Claude manages the conversation and model-driven steps, while J.Crew supplies the photography and human styling behind each recommendation. The immediate business question is whether that combination can improve complete-outfit discovery before J.Crew adds personalization based on customer history.

This is the Best Temperature Setting for Your Thermostat in Winter

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

100+ Most Unique and Practical Gifts 🎁 for Homeowners and DIYers

Published Nov 12, 2026 9:50 AM EST

We may earn revenue from the products available on this page and participate in affiliate programs. Learn More ›

Tips, tricks & ideas for a better home and yard, delivered to your inbox daily.

Following a few energy-saving strategies when setting the temperature on your thermostat can make a big difference in winter. According to the U.S. Department of Energy, it’s possible to save 10 percent a year on energy bills by being conservative with thermostat settings year-round.

We consulted an HVAC expert about thermostat-setting strategies during the coldest months of the year, and how we can save on energy costs. Here’s what he said.

“I prefer setting the thermostat to around 68 degrees Fahrenheit (20 degrees Celsius) during the day. This temperature strikes a balance between comfort and energy efficiency,” says Josh Mitchell, HVAC technician and founder of Air Conditioner Lab. “At night, lowering it to about 65 degrees Fahrenheit (18 degrees Celsius) is comfortable for sleeping and helps save on heating costs.”

According to the Department of Energy, setting your thermostat to the lowest comfortable temperature, which is typically 68 degrees Fahrenheit in the winter, helps slow down heat loss for homes with a regular furnace or baseboard heating system. However, that’s not the case for homes with heat pumps. Dialing down the thermostat too much can make a heat pump less efficient.

Everyone has a different comfort zone when it comes to temperatures. Just because 68 degrees can be an ideal temperature for a house in terms of reducing energy consumption doesn’t mean it’s right for everyone. Some people may prefer to set the thermostat slightly higher, at 70 degrees, and that’s okay.

The  Amazon Smart Thermostat uses Alexa to adjust the thermostat based on whether it thinks you’re home. Read the review.

It’s important to install a thermostat in the right location to prevent inaccurate temperature readings. Called ‘ghost readings,’ a thermostat may be triggered to run unnecessarily if placed near drafts, sunlight, and doorways. If air flow near the thermostat is blocked by furniture, also may not run effectively.

Mitchell says that the best place to locate a thermostat is on an interior wall that experiences the natural air flow in the home and is away from direct sunlight, drafts, doorways, and windows.

Your body requires time to adapt to slightly cooler temperatures. So there’s no need to (literally) go cold turkey when cooler outdoor temps arrive. Lower the temperature gradually, so you have time to adjust to being a little chilly. Until you acclimate, wear layers, don warm socks, and have a snuggly blanket on hand.

Regardless of how low you set the thermostat, you won’t save on your heating bill if your furnace or heating system works inefficiently. Make sure to do the following:

“Setting the thermostat too high in winter can lead to excessive energy consumption and higher utility bills,” Mitchell explains. “Conversely, setting it too low might not provide adequate comfort and could even risk pipes freezing. The key is to find a balanced setting that ensures comfort without overburdening your heating system.”

RELATED: Can Your Utility Company Control Your Home’s Thermostat?

Consider investing in a programmable thermostat that’ll automatically adjust the temperature for you. Programmable thermostats are ideal for people with consistent schedules.

“I’m a strong advocate for smart thermostats,” Mitchell says. “They offer advanced features like learning your schedule, remote control via a smartphone app, and energy usage reports, which not only enhances energy efficiency but also contributes to a more comfortable living environment.”

If your day-to-day routine is a bit more haphazard than a regular 9 to 5, consider a smart thermostat. Helpful features of some smart thermostats include settings that automatically detect your absence and lower the thermostat temperature while you’re gone. Some units can adjust the indoor temperature based on weather data.

“The ability to monitor and adjust your home’s temperature settings from anywhere can lead to cost savings and reduce your carbon footprint,” Mitchell explains.

Unless you work from home, turning the thermostat down during the workday can mean big savings on your energy bill. If you have pets, of course, you’ll want to set the thermostat to a temperature that will keep them comfortable while you’re gone. Still, the exact comfort zone will vary depending on the kind of pet and the animal’s size.

Another good opportunity to lower your thermostat is while you sleep. After all, research suggests that most people sleep poorly in overly warm conditions.

How Bravo Star Joey Zauzig Turned an 1,100-Square-Foot WeHo Rental Into His Postdivorce ‘Sanctuary in the Sky’

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Food influencer Joey Zauzig has worked closely with famed interior designer Jeff Lewis in broadcasting for years, yet the luxury West Hollywood apartment where he often delivers his signature saucy meal reviews and motivational speeches is one he decorated without help.

Zauzig, 34, is a fan favorite on Lewis’ SiriusXM show, “Jeff Lewis Live,” and a recurring cast member on “Still Flipping Out,” Bravo’s new spinoff of Lewis’ original “Flipping Out” series.

On both programs, Zauzig and Lewis have had their differences, but Zauzig reveals to Realtor.com® in Celebrity Sanctuary that when it comes to the style of his L.A.-area home, he believes they’re totally aligned.

“We have a similar sense of taste when it comes to homes and when it comes to renovations,” states Zauzig, while clarifying that Lewis hasn’t been by his place but is always welcome to visit. "I’ll let him see it whenever he wants to come over. I think he'll like it."

The former “The Real Friends of WeHo” star moved into the 1,100-square-foot, two-bedroom, two-bathroom rental unit located inside a high-rise residential tower in West Hollywood in October 2024. At the time, the Virginia native was seeking a safe place to start over following the breakup of his marriage and an unsettling break-in at a previous WeHo residence.

Today, Zauzig’s “small, but perfect” dwelling is his preferred space to comfortably work on his personal brand—and work on himself. It’s where he filmed one of his most popular social media posts “in a tux, shooting the sh*t, eating a shrimp cocktail in the kitchen,” and where he intentionally watched his Bravo debut alone so he could learn and grow through some serious self-reflection.

“To just be in my own home, watch it by myself, and be able to go through those emotions by myself—I was under the covers with a heating pad, face mask on,” recounts Zauzig.

“The best therapy you can ever get is to be able to watch yourself back on TV,” he continues. “I’m by no means perfect. We all make mistakes. We all have things we want to tweak about ourselves when it comes to the journey of becoming your best self. Mine got accelerated from the show, and that's a great thing. Joey 2.0 is going great.”

In this edition of Celebrity Sanctuary, Zauzig details how he’s gotten to “a good place” as he and his carefully curated West Hollywood home continue to be “works in progress.”

I really got this place because of the views. I was going through a divorce. I needed a little sanctuary in the sky, and that's what I got, and it just makes me happy because it's mine and it's beautiful.

I always had my eye on this building. A lot of cool people from my industry live here. It’s [got a] full-time security doorman, marble, light oak wood in all of the hallways. I was like, “If I move into a building in L.A., it has to be this building specifically.” I really was dead-set on that and also safety. I wanted somewhere that I felt, [as] someone that travels so much, could just pick up and leave without any worry.

I definitely made a lot of changes to the place. Farrow & Ball did all of the painting. They sent a paint specialist over. I went really dark. I did a sexy, dark-brown chocolate bedroom, and then my closet is all dark-brown lacquer. The roof is also lacquer, so it reflects [and] it looks bigger. The rest is this taupe-beige. It's a muted white. I didn’t want stark white walls, because it gives rental.

I changed a lot of light fixtures because that was important to me. There was this bubble light fixture in the kitchen, these glass bulbs. It was awful, and it was stark white light. I changed that to this Lulu and Georgia overhead, and I changed the placement of the lighting so it's over my island now, which is really nice. Then there [were] these alien-looking lights! They're not cute, so I changed those into CB2 canister lights. They're a dark black, which goes really nice with the tan [paint]. It feels more warm, cozy.

Other than that, I just made it my own. I made some custom cushions for the balcony outside.

[With] a rental, you never know if you could make changes, but there’s still some tweaks I want to do. Definitely some changes in the kitchen. I hate the green subway tiles. If I felt like putting money into this place, that's what I would change because I am in the kitchen so much. They've got to go. I’m not a subway-tile kind of guy. I would do a marble.

My family room is definitely my sanctuary because I have this white boucle chair where I sit. That's where I edit all my content.

You know when you can't wait to get home and go into that one spot that you're going to relax in, chill in, scroll for hours, watch TV? That's my family room and my specific chair. I love a swivel chair. They help my anxiety a little bit. I just can't wait to get home, get into that boucle chair. I work, I eat, I watch TV. It really is my sanctuary.

The swivel chair is vintage from Mid-Century Modern in Burbank, CA. It was my first vintage piece, and it's special to me. He re-covered it in white boucle. It's so comfortable.

I should probably separate [work and home life] better, but I like to feel comfortable when I'm doing my work, especially when I'm editing. When I need to focus, I like to be in a comfortable chair. I am a really tall guy, so I need to be chillin’ and cozy.

Once I put my phone and my work stuff away, I can actually make the sanctuary a relaxing space.

It's a mix of vintage and Design Within Reach. I have one of those Design Within Reach consoles, this really beautiful silver and glass coffee table, and a CB2 silver lamp. It goes well together with the white boucle and the Design Within Reach dark wood. Then I have a huge Lulu and Georgia Jake Arnold rug, and it's so fluffy and comfortable. It's a smaller family room, so you can have people on the floor and sitting down around the coffee table. It's a really fun little gathering area, and it's comfortable.

Then I have a huge 80-inch TV because I do watch a lot of TV, so I need it to be big. I know a lot of people say you shouldn't have the TV in the family room because it's trashy—I’ll have a TV in my family room all day long, no matter where I live. I think it's chic. I want people to know I'm a TV watcher. I think it adds a little something.

I have this [Pierre] Jeanneret chair to the right of my other chair, and that was a piece that I got as a gift to myself when I was filming my first reality show. It's an original Jeanneret chair. It has one of the red inscriptions in the back, which is one of the most rare Jeanneret chairs. The wicker part had to get redone because someone sat in the chair, broke it, and fell through, so that [part] is not original, but it's fine.

I’m going to change some things for sure soon. I need a new couch.

Just as I'm a work in progress, so is my sanctuary. It's getting there, it’s just not there. It actually makes perfect sense for where I'm at right now. We're all works in progress, and I'm becoming Joey 2.0, so I'm excited.

Square Makes Squaremates AI Agent the Main Dashboard Interface

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Square announced on November 12, 2026, that its Squaremates AI business agent is generally available to U.S. sellers and has become the main way they use Square Dashboard after signing in. New tools for connecting outside AI assistants and building seller-specific apps are also available at no additional cost.

Squaremates is designed to handle complex, multi-step work in its own secure workspace, using cloud compute to operate across more parts of a seller’s business. It builds on Managerbot, which entered open beta six months earlier with an approach in which the system monitors operations and proposes actions while the seller decides what proceeds.

The agent can review a sales period, identify lapsed customers and produce a re-engagement campaign or discount. It can also make bulk catalog changes to prices, descriptions, channel visibility and images, or compare labor costs with sales to prepare a more cost-optimized staffing schedule for review. These workflows extend beyond answering questions: Squaremates can change business data and prepare operational work for seller approval.

Completed tasks can yield usable artifacts, including graphical sales breakdowns and fully designed, on-brand email campaigns, rather than only text responses. A beta restocking feature can identify low inventory, prepare an order and build it on a supplier’s website for the seller to complete. Square also plans a daily briefing that would use permissioned email access to combine inbox content with information about the business.

The improved Square MCP (Model Context Protocol) lets sellers connect their Square account to a third-party AI assistant once, then ask questions and make changes through a defined set of Square actions. It is intended for businesses that already work with outside assistants and want those tools to interact directly with their Square operations.

The new Square CLI (Command-Line Interface) serves a more technical audience, giving sellers’ and developers’ own tools and agents command-line access to Square APIs for scripting, scheduling and building on their accounts.

Those interfaces also support Squaremates Apps, custom applications connected to a seller’s business. Sellers can describe what they need to Squaremates or use Square CLI with AI coding tools, then deploy and host the resulting apps on Square. Suggested uses include dividing cash tips according to hours worked and displaying live staffing information across locations.

Donnie McClanahan, who runs four cafes in the Knoxville, Tennessee area, used Square CLI and an AI coding agent to build a labor-board app. It combines schedules and timecards into one live view across his locations, allowing him to see who is working at each cafe without checking every site separately.

Fraser Thompson, who leads strategy and operations at Los Angeles-based BURGERS NEVER SAY DIE, used Squaremates to examine attachment rates and customer segments for its house-made cheese sauce. The analysis showed that longstanding regular customers particularly favored it, prompting social-media marketing and efforts to make the sauce more discoverable. Describing the outcome, Thompson said, “A few months later, we’d 12x’d our cheese sauce.”

Squaremates is not currently available to Multi-Account Suite sellers. Square is also planning a Squaremates mobile app built on Buzz for team communication and task management, including staff interaction with AI agents. The company is offering a waitlist for alpha access rather than general availability.

The announcement extends the operational-intelligence strategy Square began with Square AI in 2025. For sellers, the practical change is that AI can now sit both inside Dashboard and inside their own tools, with seller approval and staged feature rollouts defining how much work is handed over.

They Left Iran, but Now the Regime Is Seizing Their Assets to Silence Them

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

The Iranian authorities are pursuing diaspora dissidents and journalists, extending the government’s repressive apparatus far beyond its borders.

Yeganeh Torbati spoke to 12 Iranians living abroad whose property in Iran, or that of their family members, was targeted for confiscation.

The retired couple in Iran first realized that something was wrong when their bank cards stopped working in early May. After inquiring with the authorities, they discovered that their assets had been frozen by order of the country’s intelligence ministry.

The couple learned, eventually, that they were being punished for the actions of their daughter Noushin Najafiragheb, who lived in Europe — specifically, for her social media posts opposing the Iranian government, according to another daughter, Mehrnoush Najafiragheb.

Across Iran, the government has used asset seizures as punishment for actions taken abroad. Homes and businesses have been seized, life savings raided, family heirlooms taken. When those targeted had little inside Iran themselves, the authorities went after their family members back home, interviews show.

As the Najafiragheb family pressed their case with the authorities, Mehrnoush said, they encountered dozens of other people facing the specter of losing their life’s savings as punishment for the actions of their relatives abroad.

“We realized we weren’t alone,” Ms. Najafiragheb recalled her mother telling her. She said her sister Noushin had declined to be interviewed to avoid further government pressure on her family.

Baidu Launches Token Exchange for Verified AI Model Services

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Baidu launched Token Exchange, a marketplace for comparing and buying AI model services, at Baidu World 2026 on November 12, 2026, alongside upgrades across its models, agents, cloud and chips. The company reported that the exchange is averaging more than 50 million calls and over 700 billion tokens a day after one month.

Token Exchange gives businesses and individual users one place to compare prices and performance and then access services from multiple model providers. Its verification process compares model fingerprints and tests capability, performance and availability. Providers receive credit ratings based on regular spot checks and real-time, end-to-end call monitoring; Baidu says more than 100 model resources have been onboarded. The design combines initial verification with continuing oversight rather than treating a listing as a one-time check.

The model updates included ERNIE 5.5 Flash Preview, which Baidu released on November 11, 2026, one day before Baidu World. It is the first post-trained model built on the earlier ERNIE 5.5 Flash Base, with 280 billion total parameters, 14 billion activated per token and a 1-million-token context window. Baidu positions it for coding, 3D modeling, knowledge work and content creation.

Baidu says the Preview balances capability with inference efficiency, citing competitive benchmark scores and fast decoding. Its demonstrations emphasize work inside an agent harness: in an 11-hour Unreal Engine task, the text-only model wrote a third-person game in C++ and converted playtest problems, including a hovering character and misplaced camera, into measurable software tests because it could not see the screen.

The API is available through Qianfan at RMB 1 per million input tokens and RMB 4 per million output tokens, with cached input at RMB 0.04 per million. Baidu plans to release the weights under Apache 2.0 by November 25, 2026, while cautioning that this intermediate post-training checkpoint may still fall short in some capabilities as training continues.

Two application-specific models accompanied it. ERNIE Wizshot Micro-Expression combines text, images and speech for expressive video creation. ERNIE Light Deep Thinking handles complex search requests and tool calls by pairing a fast-thinking model that serves users with a slow-thinking model that continuously refines it. Baidu says its Sparse Attention technology compresses historical memory during decoding, reducing inference costs without compromising performance.

Robin Li, Baidu’s co-founder and CEO, framed the portfolio around a shift from fixed hierarchies to organizations built around tasks. His three principles are to break down job boundaries, convert tacit knowledge into shared organizational assets, and create measurable closed-loop processes that support continuous learning. Baidu’s internal figures provide the adoption case: average daily active agents rose from 5,000 to 37,000 over the past year, while weekly completed tasks increased from 84,000 to more than 1 million.

DuMate is now the unified gateway to Baidu’s specialized agents, and its Harness engine and Agent API have been opened to developers. The DuMate team said it iterated more than 160 times in six months and used the agent to generate 90% of the team’s code. KOOKO, upgraded from GenFlow 3.0, combines public information, professional databases and users’ own content to produce research reports, slide decks and websites.

Famou 3.0 addresses production operations by combining an agent platform with forward-deployed engineering services, with Baidu reporting customers across 25 of China’s 41 industrial categories.

Baidu AI Cloud launched the Tianchi 1024-card Supernode, an all-optical interconnect system built for high-speed data transmission. Kunlunxin, Baidu’s AI chip business, said its M100 inference chip has shipped, while the flagship M300 is expected in 2027 with improvements in computing power, memory and interconnection.

Together, the announcements connect Baidu’s silicon and cloud infrastructure to foundation models, workflow agents and a marketplace for model services. Token Exchange extends that stack beyond Baidu’s own products. The open question is whether its verification and credit ratings can turn early usage into a trusted channel for enterprises choosing among model providers.

Denial, Bargaining, Anger: Where Our Relationship With AI Goes Next

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Nearly four years after ChatGPT’s public debut, our collective relationship with AI has followed a pattern that looks a lot like the five stages of grief. What we’re mourning is the familiar, along with a sense of stability that was already hanging on by a thread before any chatbot arrived. The stages haven’t moved in a straight line, and traces of each keep resurfacing.

Early on, plenty of us were curious about what the technology could do, while skeptics denied it would ever make a real impact. Then AI moved into our daily lives and workflows, and the bargaining set in. “It will only handle the boring tasks.” “A few weeks without it won’t hurt.” “Maybe I can skip it altogether.” I’ve watched that bargaining play out in my own industry, where agency budgets keep shrinking while client demand stays the same, and AI keeps getting pitched as the way to close the gap. Running underneath every stage has been the same anxiety and fear. Now that fear is boiling over into anger.

Anger tends to show up when fear has nowhere left to go. Three things have pushed us to that point.

The first is ubiquity. A Pew Research Center survey of more than 5,000 U.S. adults found Americans are far more worried than excited about the growing role of AI in daily life. That worry is no longer abstract, because the technology sits inside our search bars, our inboxes, our classrooms, and our job descriptions.

The second is that the stakes have become personal. Early debates about AI were about capability. The current debate is about rent, tuition, and whether our kids will learn to think without a machine doing the thinking first.

The third is volume. Our feeds, ads, and customer service chats are filling up with machine-made content, and more of us can tell. Recognition breeds resentment quickly.

Pressure to regulate AI is growing, especially around how young people use it. In September 2025, the Federal Trade Commission launched an inquiry into AI chatbots acting as companions, asking several major companies how they test for and limit harm to children and teens. When a federal regulator starts asking those questions, public concern has moved well past the dinner table.

Teachers and professors describe students who hand their thinking off to AI and then struggle to recall what they submitted. The research is still early. An MIT Media Lab preprint titled Your Brain on ChatGPT found that participants who wrote essays with a language model showed weaker neural engagement and poorer recall of their own work. The study was small and had not been peer-reviewed when it was posted, so it’s best treated as a signal and not a verdict. Still, it gave educators language for something they were already seeing in their classrooms.

Fear about work is the most widespread and the most personal. Pew found that 52% of U.S. workers were worried about the future impact of AI in the workplace, 32% expected fewer job opportunities for themselves, and only 36% felt hopeful. Creative professionals feel it acutely, since their work is exactly what generative tools imitate best.

The layoff numbers add weight. Challenger, Gray & Christmas tracked 1,206,374 announced job cuts in 2025, up 58% from the year before. Not all of those cuts were about AI, and it would be unfair to say they were. But the perception matters as much as the attribution. Entire teams are being trimmed because one person knows how to pull the right levers, and many of us feel like our heads are on the guillotine.

That fear for our own futures, and for our children’s futures, is now surfacing as backlash against AI-generated content. When McDonald’s Netherlands released an AI-generated holiday ad, the reaction was fierce enough that, according to the BBC, the company pulled it. Brands that treated synthetic content as a cost-saving shortcut are discovering that audiences read it as a message about how much they’re valued.

AI has also become unavoidable. Most of us use it every day, and many of us have to just to keep up. A writer can despise what AI is doing to the industry and still run drafts through it at night. A teacher can ban it in class and quietly use it for lesson plans.

A counter-movement is forming around human-made work, basic learning principles, and in-person community, and it’s pushing back against a tool most of its own members still depend on. That isn’t hypocrisy. We can resent something and rely on it at the same time, and that is exactly what grief looks like in practice.

No one can forecast this precisely, but I see five developments as likely.

That last shift matters most in learning, where calm and attention are part of the work and not a separate problem. Schools are already acting on this. Districts across the country have added breathing exercises, short meditations, and yoga to the school day, and the CDC has endorsed mindfulness practices to help students manage stress and emotions. None of this makes mindfulness a cure-all, but it reflects a growing recognition that understanding takes time, and a rushed or anxious mind has a harder time doing it. AI makes that easy to forget. The real risk of AI-assisted learning isn’t that students get answers too easily, but that they skip the slower, more uncomfortable work of understanding why an answer is right. Protecting that work is where companies and educators can start.

Public trust will be earned through specific choices.

For educators, the goal isn’t to choose between banning AI and embracing it. It’s to make the purpose of each assignment clear, so students understand what the exercise is meant to build and why a shortcut defeats it.

The anger around AI is easy to dismiss as resistance to progress. I think it’s more accurate to read it as grief, and as a sign that we care about what we stand to lose. Many of us already sense how AI enriches us, personally and professionally, and how it depletes us, professionally, socioeconomically, and even financially. We’re still discovering where that balance lands.

That balance won’t settle on its own. Technology can look neutral, but it’s never without consequence. The way it’s built shapes how it gets used, and some of its effects are unintended, taking years to fully grasp and understand. That’s why it matters whether a tool is designed to take from people or to nurture them. The organizations that earn public trust will be the ones that choose to nurture, and that protect their teams as much as their customers. They’ll keep the humanity of the work at the center, because people are who it’s for.

If we let this grief run its course, what comes after anger won’t be surrender. It will be acceptance built on knowledge: understanding AI well enough to know when it serves us, when it takes from us, and when to set it aside. Turning that knowledge into wisdom is the work of the next year.

Michael Verez Mora is the founder and CEO of Yellow Lion (yellowlion.nyc), a New York-based brand and digital creative agency working across technology, entertainment, fintech, and beyond. He is also the co-founder of Caldo (caldo.app), a learning and mindfulness app built on his team’s proprietary AI-based technology, where he focuses on building tools that support how people learn and think.

In China’s Spy Operations, Romance Can Be a Long Game

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Jensen Huang’s affair with a woman U.S. officials say is a Chinese spy has drawn attention to the role of intimate relationships in espionage.

At the height of the Cold War, a top official in China’s intelligence agency said that his country would never use seduction to entrap a person.

Such tactics might be for the C.I.A. or the K.G.B., the official named Kong Yuan, of the Central Investigation Department, told a group of agents in training in April 1960, but China was guided by principles. He looked at the few female comrades in the audience. “Rest assured, we will not be asking you to go run beauty traps.”

Despite that pledge, captured in a 2010 memoir by a former Chinese diplomat, China’s intelligence services have for decades used romantic entanglements to compromise or extract information from prominent people.

American officials say that China’s Ministry of State Security, which runs intelligence operations today, recruited a Chinese businesswoman to collect intelligence on Jensen Huang, the chief executive of Nvidia and one of the most powerful figures in the artificial intelligence boom.

It was not clear what role the woman, Alice Huang, or Huang Le, might have had with the Chinese spy agency. Ms. Huang was a top executive of a Singapore company that operates data centers in Southeast Asia using Nvidia chips and that rents their computing power to Chinese firms.

Mr. Huang has acknowledged that he had an affair with Ms. Huang. President Trump has said that Mr. Huang told him that he was not aware that the woman was a spy. China has rejected the espionage allegations against Ms. Huang, calling them smears against the country. U.S. officials have not provided evidence for their claims. Ms. Huang has not responded to requests for comment.

And, even though they've spent around £600 million on new players over the last couple of years, more change is needed which is why the January transfer window could end up being a huge deal for them.

Liverpool can re-ignite their interest in Bilal El Khannouss

Most areas of Liverpool's squad could do with a new player being added to it.

Their defence was supposed to be one of their strongest areas this term but, the performances of Milos Kerkez and Kostas Tsimikas have made it clear that neither of them are suited to being the Reds' first-choice left-back.

And, while some of their previous links have made little sense, El Khannouss isn't one of them.

In the end, Wirtz was the midfielder chosen by Liverpool but, since he's failed to meet expectations, it's clear to see why a deal for El Khannouss should be considered.

He'd only end up costing Liverpool around £35 million which, in this day and age, is a bargain.

And, since he's already contributed to 46 goals across his career and has already spent time in the Premier League, he could be a perfect purchase for Iraola.

To provide shoppers with a list of top picks for snow thrower and snow blower oils, we primarily considered oil type and viscosity.

Oil type: Synthetic oil is the best choice for snow blower motors because it can handle lower temperatures and resist breakdown better than conventional oil.

Viscosity: Our recommendations are all variations of 5W-30, which is the ideal oil viscosity for snow blowers. The products we selected include budget-friendly versions for occasional use and heavier-duty options that come at a slightly higher price point. Most of the picks on our list are also versatile enough for other home and garden equipment and suitable for both cold and hot weather conditions.

The right oil can make the difference between a snow blower not starting because it has a gummed-up engine and one that kicks on without issue. Ahead, learn how viscosity, snow blower oil type, and other factors affect engine performance in a snow blower.

Gas-powered snow blowers use 4-cycle engine oil, a type of oil for snow blower engines that uses separate tanks for gas and oil, much like car engines. The gas fuels the engine, while the oil lubricates it.

Smaller engines, such as those on chainsaws, have 2-cycle engines. This type of small engine uses an oil and gas mixture that goes into one tank. Don’t use 2-cycle engine oil in a snow blower, as it will not properly lubricate the engine and will damage it. The motors on electric snow blowers don’t require engine oil.

Identifying the right type of engine oil is easy. The oil container has “4-cycle” or “2-cycle” clearly printed on the label. Although some 4-cycle engine oils are labeled for “small engines,” they are actually the same oil commonly used in cars and trucks.

Viscosity refers to the thickness of the oil. Higher viscosity oil is better at protecting and lubricating engines at high temperatures and peak load (high RPMs). Oil with a low viscosity remains thinner at lower temperatures, promoting better flow through the engine, making it easier to start and more efficient to run.

Viscosity is based on a scale created by SAE International, formerly known as the Society of Automotive Engineers (SAE). Motor oil has two viscosity ratings:

Since snow blowers operate exclusively in cold weather, they typically use SAE 5W-30 weight oil, which ensures the engine will start at low temperatures. Make sure to check the maintenance section of your operating manual for the proper snow blower oil weight for your machine.

Not all 5W-30 engine oils are the same. Some engine oils are synthetic, which means they use higher-quality base oils than conventional oils. Synthetic lubricants can generally withstand higher temperatures and resist breakdown better. This means synthetic oil in a snow blower will last at least 50 percent longer than conventional oil, so you’ll probably need to perform less frequent snow blower maintenance.

Many synthetic oils also contain additives to help improve engine performance and extend engine life. Additives include detergents that flush grime and buildup and chemicals that coat engine parts to enhance lubrication.

For these reasons, synthetic oils are generally more expensive, often costing several times more than conventional oils. Still, proper maintenance of a quality snow blower will likely run you less than the cost of snow removal if you live in an area with relatively frequent snowfalls.

The 4-cycle engine oil that snow blowers use is the same oil used in any 4-cycle engine, which means it’s also an appropriate oil for lawn mowers, power washers, and even cars and trucks. 5W-30 oil provides adequate protection during both cold and warm weather.

Engine oil generally comes in 1-quart bottles, which is ideal for snow blowers since they hold about that much. It also comes in 5-quart containers and large, multi-gallon drums, but since its shelf life is generally around 5 years, it’s important not to buy more than you need. To store 5W-30 oil, keep it tightly sealed in a cool, dry environment.

Even though you now know a fair bit about oil for snow blowers, you may still have additional questions. For details on deciding which type of oil in a snow blower is best to use and how often to replace it, read answers to some frequently asked questions below.

Yes, synthetic options are likely the best oils for snow blowers. Synthetic oils are refined to run cleaner, leaving fewer deposits in the engine. Many also include additives that clean grime and sludge from the engine to improve performance.

Yes. Snow blowers, like cars, are 4-cycle engines, so they use the same type of motor oil. Use a motor oil weight with a low-temperature viscosity rating, such as 5W-30, since snow blowers must run during cold weather.

Snow blowers operate in frigid temperatures, so the most important factor to consider is viscosity. Use motor oil with a low-temperature viscosity rating, such as 5W-30, to ensure the snow blower starts easily and runs smoothly.

Adding too much oil to a snow blower, or any 4-cycle engine, will cause the oil to rise and come in contact with other moving parts around the engine, aerating the oil and creating a huge mess.

According to Chris Urato, owner-operator of Urato Landscaping, LLC, you should perform a snow blower oil change “after the first 5 hours of use for new snow blowers, then every 20 to 50 hours of operation or once per season, depending on usage.”

A well-maintained snow blower should last around 10 to 15 years, but actual lifespan will depend on build quality and frequency of use. Performing regular maintenance tasks and avoiding common snow blower mistakes will help your machine last as long as possible.

“Yes, running it dry prevents stale fuel from gumming up the carburetor and causing maintenance issues,” says Chris Urato, a landscaper and snow-removal provider from Goshen, New York.

If you have a gas snow blower, you can give it 2 to 5 minutes before use so oil has time to run through the engine and lubricate it. If you have a battery-powered blower, there’s no need to let it warm up.

The company's founder stepped down after reports of multiple affairs.

Jensen Huang has resigned as CEO of $4 trillion chip giant Nvidia, just days after news broke that he was having an affair with a Chinese data center executive alleged to have ties to China’s intelligence community.

The company named Colette Kress, its chief financial officer, as interim CEO. Nvidia confirmed the news in a press release Thursday morning.

As reported Wednesday by The Wall Street Journal, the removal of Huang as CEO follows the revelation over the weekend that the company’s board of directors had opened an investigation into whether the company’s chief executive and founder had been targeted by foreign intelligence.

Huang had been revealed to be having an affair with Alice Huang (no relation), a Chinese citizen and executive at Megaspeed, a Singapore-based data center company that buys chips from Nvidia to run data centers that sell computing power to Chinese AI labs. News reports citing US intelligence officials have claimed Alice Huang was working as a spy for China. She was reportedly under surveillance by US intelligence, as reported in late October by The New York Times.

The Information reported on Tuesday that Alice Huang is believed to be involved with a network of Chinese companies that facilitated the sales of $7.5 billion in chips to China. The US has tried to control China’s access to advanced chips for years, but reports show that there are still illegal operations smuggling chips on the banned list from the US to China.

The New York Times also reported on Wednesday a separate affair between Huang and one of his direct reports at Nvidia.

The announcement of an investigation has rocked the AI chip behemoth, which makes the cutting-edge chips that enable AI and AI data centers. A proposed class-action lawsuit was filed on Monday against Nvidia, charging the company made false or misleading statements about Huang’s affair and the company’s relationship with Megaspeed.

On Tuesday, the Nvidia board said it had “retained former acting US Attorney for the Southern District of New York Joon H. Kim and his firm Wachtell, Lipton, Rosen & Katz to conduct a thorough and independent investigation.”

BlackRock’s IBIT Bleeds $2 Billion in Nine Straight Days of Outflows

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

BlackRock’s iShares Bitcoin Trust (IBIT) lost another $269.7 million on Wednesday, its ninth straight session of redemptions, as U.S. spot bitcoin exchange-traded funds posted a fifth consecutive day of net outflows totaling $299.8 million.

Across the nine sessions since Oct. 30, IBIT has shed about $2 billion. The fund, the largest of the U.S. spot bitcoin ETFs, has seen money leave on every trading day so far in November.

The broader group has fared only slightly better. The 11 funds have lost about $1.44 billion over the past five trading days and roughly $1.88 billion in November, with outflows on seven of the month’s eight sessions. The lone exception was Nov. 4, when $74.6 million came in, even as IBIT itself bled $201.2 million that day.

IBIT accounts for about $1.77 billion of November’s outflows, or roughly 94% of the total.

Wednesday’s selling was concentrated. Besides IBIT, Grayscale’s GBTC lost $39.1 million, while Morgan Stanley’s MSBT was the only fund to take in money, about $9 million. The rest of the funds reported no flows.

The current run is not a record. Both the group and IBIT went 13 straight trading days without an inflow from May 15 to June 3, the longest outflow streaks of 2026. IBIT followed that with an 11-session streak from June 17 to July 2.

Still, the size of the recent withdrawals stands out. IBIT’s two largest daily outflows of the year both came this month: $405.3 million on Nov. 2 and $403 million on Nov. 6.

The selling has also flipped the year. U.S. spot bitcoin ETFs are now down about $1.42 billion in net flows for 2026, compared with roughly $25.2 billion of inflows at the same point last year. The Australian Financial Review noted Thursday that the selling has erased this year’s net inflows into the funds.

IBIT is still positive for the year, with about $2.45 billion in net inflows in 2026 and roughly $64.5 billion since it launched in January 2024. That cushion has shrunk quickly. IBIT took in about $932 million in October, and November’s withdrawals have already erased that amount nearly twice over.

The withdrawals come just days after BlackRock argued that security fears were driving bitcoin whales out of self-custody and into IBIT.

The outflows began before this week’s worst headlines but have picked up alongside them. A hotter-than-expected October inflation report on Tuesday lifted bets on a Federal Reserve rate hike in December, and Polymarket odds of a hike climbed to about 97%, Yahoo Finance reported. Higher rates make cash and bonds more attractive relative to assets like bitcoin.

Tech stocks have added pressure. Nvidia shares fell roughly 9% after Wednesday’s close following reports that its board was preparing to remove CEO Jensen Huang, and Nasdaq 100 futures slid with them. Nvidia has not announced a decision. Bitcoin has traded closely with tech shares this year.

Index provider MSCI also confirmed after Wednesday’s close that it will remove Strategy (MSTR) from its indexes after Nov. 30, a decision that forces tracking funds to sell shares of the largest corporate bitcoin holder.

Bitcoin traded near $72,800 early Thursday, close to Tuesday’s low of about $72,700, its weakest level since Aug. 20. It is down roughly 6% over the past week.

ETF flows for Thursday will be reported after the close. The test now is whether IBIT snaps its streak before it matches the 13-day run from the spring, or whether the steady redemptions keep weighing on the bitcoin price heading into the Fed’s December meeting.

Bitcoin Price Falls Below $72,000 as Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang Steps Down and Strategy (MSTR) Slides

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Bitcoin price fell below $72,000 on Thursday morning, its lowest level since mid-August, after Nvidia said CEO Jensen Huang had resigned and Strategy shares slid following MSCI’s confirmation of an index exit, according to Bitcoin Magazine Pro data.

The selling started after Wednesday’s close. Bitcoin slipped from about $74,700 to near $73,400 by 6 p.m. ET, then drifted sideways overnight. A second leg lower began around 6 a.m., and the bitcoin price briefly dipped to about $72,200 shortly after 8 a.m. The decline accelerated later in the morning, and bitcoin traded as low as about $71,670 around 9:29 a.m.

That move pushed the bitcoin price below the low near $72,700 it set after Tuesday’s hotter-than-expected inflation report. It now trades roughly $31,200 below where it stood one year ago.

Nvidia (NVDA) said Thursday that Huang has resigned as president, chief executive and a member of the board, effective immediately. The company named Chief Financial Officer Colette Kress as interim CEO and appointed Harvey C. Jones as its first board chair. Kress will continue as chief financial officer while she serves in the interim role. The board is searching for a permanent successor and said it will consider internal and external candidates. Nvidia is still scheduled to report fiscal third-quarter results on Nov. 17.

The resignation follows reports of extramarital relationships, including one that has raised national security concerns in Washington. Sen. Kevin Cramer, a North Dakota Republican, had told CNBC that Huang should step down, saying he was “so vulnerable to extortion, blackmail.”

Nvidia shares fell about 13% at Thursday’s open. Nasdaq 100 futures were down about 1.4% and S&P 500 futures fell about 0.7%, while Dow futures were nearly flat. Intel, Marvell Technology and Micron were down between about 5% and 7.5%, and CoreWeave dropped about 16%, giving back most of Wednesday’s 24.7% surge. Data center firms IREN, Applied Digital and Core Scientific also traded lower.

Bitcoin price has moved with tech for most of this month. It fell on Monday when an earlier Nvidia probe rattled markets, then lagged Wednesday’s rebound in chip stocks. Thursday’s slide wiped out that small gain and then some.

Strategy (MSTR), the largest corporate holder of bitcoin, fell about 7% at the open after MSCI confirmed it will remove the stock from its indexes. MSCI’s November review removed Strategy from the MSCI USA Index, a step that also takes it out of the MSCI World and ACWI indexes, effective at the Nov. 30 close. Japan’s Metaplanet is also slated for removal.

Index funds will sell MSTR shares, not bitcoin, and nothing in the rules requires Strategy to sell its coins. Still, a weaker stock makes it harder for the company to raise money for new purchases at a time when miners such as MARA are selling coins.

Fund demand stayed weak. Spot bitcoin ETFs lost $299.8 million on Wednesday, their fifth straight day of net outflows, bringing the five-day total to about $1.44 billion. BlackRock’s IBIT accounted for $269.7 million of Wednesday’s redemptions, its ninth straight session of outflows.

This morning’s labor data did not ease the rate outlook. Initial jobless claims rose to 200,000 last week, above the 195,000 economists had expected, the Labor Department said. Traders still see a December Fed rate hike as a certainty, with about a 10% chance of a half-point move, CME FedWatch shows. Higher rates make an asset that pays no interest harder to hold, and that has kept a lid on the bitcoin price all week.

The next test comes quickly. Cisco and Applied Materials report results after Thursday’s close, and Friday’s Producer Price Index will give investors another read on inflation after this week’s consumer price data came in hotter than expected.

At the time of writing, the bitcoin price is near $71,800.

NVIDIA Names Colette Kress Interim CEO Following Huang’s Resignation

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

NVIDIA announced on November 12, 2026 that Jensen Huang had resigned as president, chief executive officer and director, effective immediately. The board appointed Chief Financial Officer Colette Kress as interim CEO and Harvey Jones as chair.

The board accepted Huang’s resignation and made both appointments on November 11, 2026, the date recorded in NVIDIA’s Form 8-K. Kress will remain executive vice president and CFO while serving as interim CEO until a permanent successor is named. The board is initiating a search that will consider internal and external candidates.

Kress, 59, joined NVIDIA in September 2013 and has served as executive vice president and CFO since then. Before NVIDIA, she spent nearly 25 years in finance roles at technology companies including Cisco and Microsoft. NVIDIA says she has led its finance organization and played a key role in the company’s growth and evolution.

Jones, 73, has served on NVIDIA’s board since 1993 and has been managing partner of Square Wave Ventures, a private family office, since 2004. NVIDIA describes him as its first-ever chair and as an entrepreneur, technology executive and venture investor with more than 30 years of experience, including service as CEO and executive chairman of Synopsys. His appointment gives a long-serving director formal responsibility for board leadership during the succession process.

Jones said the board expects operations, customer commitments and the product roadmap to continue without interruption. Its confidence rests on Kress’s understanding of NVIDIA’s business, customers, operations and financial position and on an experienced leadership team that has helped execute the company’s strategy and advance its technology roadmap.

Kress said NVIDIA’s strength comes from its people and pointed to the foundation Huang created over 33 years. “We will continue advancing the work that has made NVIDIA a leader in accelerated computing and AI, and execute on the opportunities ahead. Our focus remains on serving our customers, partners, developers and shareholders,” she said.

The continuity message connects succession to execution, extending beyond the chief executive office to customer commitments and the technology roadmap. The appointments also separate interim executive management from board leadership, with Kress responsible for the former and Jones for the latter.

NVIDIA said it will report third-quarter fiscal 2027 results on November 17, 2026 at approximately 1:20 p.m. Pacific Time, followed by its earnings call at 2 p.m., as previously scheduled. The announcement therefore keeps the next scheduled results release and call on their existing timetable.

The results release and call are the next scheduled earnings checkpoint after the leadership change. The immediate test of the transition is whether NVIDIA can deliver the operational and product continuity the board expects while Kress holds both the interim CEO and CFO roles and the permanent-CEO search remains open.
